### Recycling the Web: A Method to Enhance Pre-training Data Quality and Quantity for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04689v1)
- **Authors**: Thao Nguyen, Yang Li, Olga Golovneva, Luke Zettlemoyer, Sewoong Oh, Ludwig Schmidt, Xian Li
- **Abstract**: Scaling laws predict that the performance of large language models improves with increasing model size and data size. In practice, pre-training has been relying on massive web crawls, using almost all data sources publicly available on the internet so far. However, this pool of natural data does not grow at the same rate as the compute supply. Furthermore, the availability of high-quality texts is even more limited: data filtering pipelines often remove up to 99% of the initial web scrapes to achieve state-of-the-art. To address the "data wall" of pre-training scaling, our work explores ways to transform and recycle data discarded in existing filtering processes. We propose REWIRE, REcycling the Web with guIded REwrite, a method to enrich low-quality documents so that they could become useful for training. This in turn allows us to increase the representation of synthetic data in the final pre-training set. Experiments at 1B, 3B and 7B scales of the DCLM benchmark show that mixing high-quality raw texts and our rewritten texts lead to 1.0, 1.3 and 2.5 percentage points improvement respectively across 22 diverse tasks, compared to training on only filtered web data. Training on the raw-synthetic data mix is also more effective than having access to 2x web data. Through further analysis, we demonstrate that about 82% of the mixed in texts come from transforming lower-quality documents that would otherwise be discarded. REWIRE also outperforms related approaches of generating synthetic data, including Wikipedia-style paraphrasing, question-answer synthesizing and knowledge extraction. These results suggest that recycling web texts holds the potential for being a simple and effective approach for scaling pre-training data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Recycling the Web: A Method to Enhance Pre-training Data Quality and Quantity for Language Models" addresses the challenge of limited high-quality data for pre-training large language models (LLMs).  It proposes REWIRE (REcycling the Web with gulded REwrite), a method that enriches moderate-quality web documents by using an LLM to rewrite and improve them. This allows the incorporation of data that would otherwise be discarded by standard filtering pipelines.  Experiments using the DCLM benchmark at 1B, 3B, and 7B model scales show that mixing high-quality raw texts with REWIRE-generated texts improves performance across a diverse set of tasks compared to training only on filtered web data. The authors demonstrate that a significant portion of the mixed-in texts originate from transformed lower-quality documents. REWIRE also outperforms other synthetic data generation approaches like Wikipedia-style paraphrasing, question-answer synthesis, and knowledge extraction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its approach to addressing the data bottleneck. While synthetic data generation and curated datasets are existing strategies, REWIRE combines both by focusing on *recycling* discarded web data.  The specific method of using a powerful LLM to rewrite lower-quality documents based on identifying its purpose and employing chain-of-thought reasoning before rewriting is also a key contribution. This guided rewriting is different from simple paraphrasing. This is a novel approach which helps scale the LLM’s without running out of high quality data.

* **Significance:** The paper is significant because it directly tackles a major problem in LLM development: the diminishing returns and limitations of relying solely on curated high-quality data. REWIRE offers a practical and scalable solution, potentially enabling better LLMs with existing data resources. The results show that REWIRE’s synthetic data can improve performance up to levels of 2x more raw data. The impact extends to organizations that may have limited access to larger curated datasets.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The paper presents a thorough set of experiments across multiple model scales and tasks using the DCLM benchmark. The results consistently demonstrate the benefits of REWIRE.
    * **Ablation Studies and Comparisons:**  The authors perform ablation studies to understand the origin of the performance gains (showing that most of the data comes from recycled documents) and compare REWIRE against other synthetic data generation methods.
    * **Qualitative Analysis:**  The paper includes qualitative analysis and examples illustrating the types of improvements made by REWIRE, such as adding knowledge or modifying the style.
    * **Addresses a timely problem:** The paper addresses a problem in LLMs and offers a useful and potentially widely-adopted approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on a powerful LLM:** The effectiveness of REWIRE depends on the performance of the LLM used for rewriting. The high computational cost of running the process at scale can be a burden.
    * **Limited Scope of "Low Quality" Definition:** The definition of "low quality" is limited. The data has already passed some RefinedWeb heuristics; REWIRE is operating on data that is *moderately* good. It is unclear how well REWIRE could transform very bad data (spam, gibberish) into useful training samples.
    * **Focus on General Domain:** The paper focuses on a general pre-training setting. It would be valuable to explore the effectiveness of REWIRE in more specialized domains where data scarcity is even more pronounced. For example, how does REWIRE perform in data sparse domains like biomedical or legal texts?
    * **Lack of negative results:** The paper doesn’t present any scenarios or task where REWIRE does worse.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research directions in pre-training data curation. It encourages a shift from solely focusing on filtering to actively transforming and improving existing web data. REWIRE could also be combined with other data augmentation techniques to further enhance pre-training datasets.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field of LLM pre-training. It presents a novel and empirically validated method for addressing the data bottleneck.  While there are weaknesses related to the dependence on a powerful LLM, computational cost and limited definition of low quality data, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The empirical results are compelling and the analysis provides useful insights. Therefore, I assign it a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Better Generalization via Distributional Input Projection Network
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04690v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Hao, Yanxin Lu, Xinwei Shen, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: As overparameterized models become increasingly prevalent, training loss alone offers limited insight into generalization performance. While smoothness has been linked to improved generalization across various settings, directly enforcing smoothness in neural networks remains challenging. To address this, we introduce Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel framework that projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer. This distributional representation induces a smoother loss landscape with respect to the input, promoting better generalization. We provide theoretical analysis showing that DIPNet reduces both local smoothness measures and the Lipschitz constant of the network, contributing to improved generalization performance. Empirically, we validate DIPNet across a wide range of architectures and tasks, including Vision Transformers (ViTs), Large Language Models (LLMs), ResNet and MLPs. Our method consistently enhances test performance under standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, and reasoning benchmarks. We demonstrate that the proposed input projection strategy can be seamlessly integrated into existing models, providing a general and effective approach for boosting generalization performance in modern deep learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Towards Better Generalization via Distributional Input Projection Network":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Distributional Input Projection Networks (DIPNet), a novel framework to enhance generalization in deep learning models. DIPNet projects inputs into learnable distributions at each layer, inducing a smoother loss landscape and promoting better generalization. The authors provide theoretical analysis showing that DIPNet reduces local smoothness measures and the Lipschitz constant. Empirically, they validate DIPNet across a variety of architectures and tasks (Vision Transformers, Large Language Models, ResNet, MLPs), demonstrating improved test performance under standard settings, adversarial attacks, out-of-distribution inputs, and reasoning benchmarks. DIPNet can be seamlessly integrated into existing models, offering a general and effective approach to boost generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of projecting inputs into learnable distributions layer-wise is reasonably novel. While previous work has explored noise injection and smoothing techniques, DIPNet's structured stochasticity and layer-wise application differentiate it. The combination of this architectural modification with a training procedure inspired by variational inference is also a positive aspect.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: generalization in overparameterized deep learning models. The results show consistent improvements across diverse architectures and tasks. The gains in adversarial robustness and out-of-distribution generalization are particularly significant, as these are often challenging areas. The fact that DIPNet can be integrated into existing models is a major strength, making it potentially widely applicable. The theoretical justifications are a good addition but could benefit from further depth and clarity on practical implications.  However, the smoothness argument relies on strong assumptions about data distribution.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of generalization and smoothness in deep learning.
*   **Novel Approach:** DIPNet presents a unique and well-defined method for improving generalization.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments cover a wide range of models and tasks, consistently demonstrating improved performance.
*   **Theoretical Justification:** The theoretical analysis provides some support for the effectiveness of DIPNet, although further elaboration would be advantageous.
*   **Easy Integration:** The architecture design allows for easy integration with existing models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Theoretical Depth:**  While the theory offers some insights, it could be strengthened with more practical implications. The distributional assumptions are strong and may not always hold in real data.
*   **Limited Ablation studies:** While the paper attempts to explore several aspects, more in-depth ablation studies about individual components are missing.
*   **Comparison to SOTA could be stronger:** While the method is compared to SAM and RS, comparing to recent more complex SOTA methods (e.g., using recent regularization strategies) would be ideal.
*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written, but certain sections could benefit from further clarification, particularly regarding the theoretical analysis and implementation details.

**Potential Impact:** DIPNet has the potential to significantly impact the field by providing a practical and effective method for improving generalization in deep learning. Its ease of integration makes it attractive for adoption, and its strong performance across a range of tasks suggests broad applicability.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 7.  While the paper presents a reasonably novel approach with strong empirical results that could influence the field, there are areas for improvement, such as theoretical depth, more exhaustive ablation, and stronger integration with SOTA baselines. There is also a risk that gains are highly dependent on the specific datasets and settings used in the paper. Therefore, while the framework is intriguing and empirically validated, further research is necessary to fully realize its theoretical understanding and applicability in real-world scenarios. The theoretical justification, while present, could also be strengthened.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cracking the Code: Enhancing Implicit Hate Speech Detection through Coding Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04693v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Wei, Liangzhi Li, Tong Xiang, Xiao Liu, Noa Garcia
- **Abstract**: The internet has become a hotspot for hate speech (HS), threatening societal harmony and individual well-being. While automatic detection methods perform well in identifying explicit hate speech (ex-HS), they struggle with more subtle forms, such as implicit hate speech (im-HS). We tackle this problem by introducing a new taxonomy for im-HS detection, defining six encoding strategies named codetypes. We present two methods for integrating codetypes into im-HS detection: 1) prompting large language models (LLMs) directly to classify sentences based on generated responses, and 2) using LLMs as encoders with codetypes embedded during the encoding process. Experiments show that the use of codetypes improves im-HS detection in both Chinese and English datasets, validating the effectiveness of our approach across different languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of detecting implicit hate speech (im-HS), which is often missed by methods designed for explicit hate speech.  The authors propose a new taxonomy of "codetypes," representing six encoding strategies used in im-HS. They then integrate these codetypes into im-HS detection using two methods: (1) prompting large language models (LLMs) directly with codetypes and (2) using LLMs as encoders with codetypes embedded during the encoding process. Experiments conducted on both Chinese and English datasets demonstrate that incorporating codetypes improves im-HS detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the taxonomy of codetypes specifically designed for *implicit* hate speech and the methods for integrating them into LLM-based detection.  Prior work has addressed hate speech classification, but this paper attempts to go deeper into the specific rhetorical devices that make im-HS more subtle and challenging to detect. This codetype taxonomy focuses on rhetorical strategies like Metaphor, Irony, and Abbreviation that are specifically designed to encode implicit hate.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles an important problem. im-HS can be more insidious and harder to moderate than explicit hate speech.  Improved detection methods could have a significant positive impact on online discourse and community well-being. The multilingual nature of the experiments (Chinese and English) is a strength, suggesting that the approach might have broader applicability.  The methods for incorporating codetypes into LLMs offer practical approaches for improving existing detection systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of im-HS detection and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Well-Defined Taxonomy:** The codetype taxonomy provides a structured framework for analyzing im-HS.  The authors describe a systematic process for identifying and refining the categories.
    *   **Multilingual Experiments:** Validating the approach on both Chinese and English datasets adds robustness to the findings.
    *   **Practical Methods:** The prompt-based and embedding-based methods offer different ways to integrate codetypes into LLM-based systems.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The authors explore the effectiveness of different codetype combinations and analyze the performance of different models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Codetype Selection:** While the authors present a method for refining the taxonomy, the initial selection of codetype candidates could be influenced by the researchers' biases. Further validation of the taxonomy by independent researchers could strengthen the results.
    *   **LLM Dependence:** The study relies heavily on LLMs. Performance might vary depending on the specific LLM used, and the generalizability of the findings needs further investigation.
    *   **Context Limitations:** The focus is on encoding, less on surrounding context. Many cases of implicit hate speech are inherently context-dependent. A better integration of contextual information (user history, dialogue structure etc.) could improve performance.
    *   **Limited Scope of 'Implicit':** The focus is mainly on rhetorical strategies, not all forms of im-HS. The taxonomy excludes several forms of implicit hate as outlined by other work.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in hate speech detection. The codetype taxonomy and integration methods could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The emphasis on implicit forms of hate speech is valuable for advancing the field.

*   **Room for Improvement:**

    *   A more robust demonstration of the model's effectiveness across various types of implicit hate, going beyond just encoding strategies, would strengthen the paper.
    *   An analysis of the errors made by the models with and without codetypes would offer valuable insights into the specific types of im-HS that remain challenging to detect.
    *   A comparison to state-of-the-art methods specific to im-HS detection would contextualize the performance of the proposed approach.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a structured way to address the detection of implicit hate speech. The codetype taxonomy and integration methods are novel and show promise. However, the reliance on LLMs, the limitations in the scope of "implicit," and the lack of comparison with state-of-the-art baselines temper the overall impact. Further refinement of the taxonomy and models, along with a broader range of experiments, would increase the paper's significance.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Empowering Economic Simulation for Massively Multiplayer Online Games through Generative Agent-Based Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04699v1)
- **Authors**: Bihan Xu, Shiwei Zhao, Runze Wu, Zhenya Huang, Jiawei Wang, Zhipeng Hu, Kai Wang, Haoyu Liu, Tangjie Lv, Le Li, Changjie Fan, Xin Tong, Jiangze Han
- **Abstract**: Within the domain of Massively Multiplayer Online (MMO) economy research, Agent-Based Modeling (ABM) has emerged as a robust tool for analyzing game economics, evolving from rule-based agents to decision-making agents enhanced by reinforcement learning. Nevertheless, existing works encounter significant challenges when attempting to emulate human-like economic activities among agents, particularly regarding agent reliability, sociability, and interpretability. In this study, we take a preliminary step in introducing a novel approach using Large Language Models (LLMs) in MMO economy simulation. Leveraging LLMs' role-playing proficiency, generative capacity, and reasoning aptitude, we design LLM-driven agents with human-like decision-making and adaptability. These agents are equipped with the abilities of role-playing, perception, memory, and reasoning, addressing the aforementioned challenges effectively. Simulation experiments focusing on in-game economic activities demonstrate that LLM-empowered agents can promote emergent phenomena like role specialization and price fluctuations in line with market rules.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to simulating economic activity in Massively Multiplayer Online (MMO) games using Generative Agent-Based Modeling (GABM) powered by Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors argue that existing Agent-Based Modeling (ABM) techniques in MMO economic simulation suffer from limitations in reliability, sociability, and interpretability. They address these limitations by leveraging the role-playing proficiency, generative capacity, and reasoning aptitude of LLMs to create LLM-driven agents capable of human-like decision-making and adaptability. The proposed framework, MMOAgent, incorporates modules for profile design (data-driven), perception, reasoning, memory, and action. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the framework through experiments showcasing emergent phenomena like role specialization and price fluctuations that align with market rules, suggesting improved realism compared to traditional ABM methods. A contribution of the paper is P2P trading, featuring direct linguistic negotiation and bargaining between players.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating LLMs into ABM for MMO economic simulation exhibits a significant degree of novelty. While generative agents have been explored in other contexts (e.g., social simulation, strategy games), the specific application to simulating MMO economies is relatively unexplored, particularly with the emphasis on P2P trading via linguistic negotiation. The data-driven approach for constructing agent profiles from real-world player data adds another layer of innovation, aiming to improve the realism and credibility of the simulation.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this research is significant. More realistic and interpretable economic simulations could offer valuable insights for game developers and policymakers. This includes: enabling them to better balance in-game economies, predict the impact of policy interventions, and design more engaging and sustainable virtual worlds. The emphasis on sociability, via linguistic interaction, is an important step forward, addressing a gap in many existing economic simulations. However, some might argue whether LLMs are appropriate when other, less computationally intensive models could perform as well.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing ABM approaches and provides a strong rationale for the use of LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive framework:** The MMOAgent framework is well-structured and integrates several key components (profile, perception, reasoning, memory, action) in a logical manner.
    *   **Data-driven approach:** The use of real-world player data to generate agent profiles is a significant strength, enhancing the realism of the simulation.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the potential of the framework to generate realistic economic phenomena. Comparisons against baselines and ablation studies provide further evidence of the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **P2P Trading**: Integrating a feature to enable P2P trading is important to better reflect real life MMO markets.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost and Hallucination:** The heavy reliance on LLMs has a significant trade-off. The computational cost is substantial. Moreover, the inherent issues of LLM hallucination pose a serious challenge, as the LLM may generate illogical or impossible actions. Though the proposed verification step helps avoid execution of invalid actions, it does not remove the fact that LLM is attempting these actions. It would be interesting to benchmark against simpler rule-based models with noise and demonstrate substantial value added.
    *   **Limited Scale:** The experiment in section 5.3.1 is only conducted with 30 players, this is not reflective of a real MMO economy.
    *   **Lack of generalizability evidence:** It is unknown whether it can be transferred to any other MMO.
    *   **Lack of comparison to other LLM-ABM agents:** Existing works have studied other LLM-ABM agents. How does MMOAgent compare to these other models (specifically regarding MMO and P2P trading)?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could inspire further research on the use of LLMs in economic simulation and game design. It could also influence the development of more sophisticated tools for game developers to analyze and balance in-game economies. The data-driven profile design approach has broad applicability beyond MMOs, as realistic agent profile generation will be useful in other applications.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a promising approach to a challenging problem, but also has a few issues that need to be resolved (hallucination, limited scale, etc). The novelty lies in a successful integration of LLMs into a specific type of ABM and the improvements provided relative to previous approaches. While computationally expensive and facing reliability concerns, the method has potential to generate interesting emergent behavior within complex virtual environments.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme for phoneme-based speech recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04711v1)
- **Authors**: Te Ma, Min Bi, Saierdaer Yusuyin, Hao Huang, Zhijian Ou
- **Abstract**: In automatic speech recognition (ASR), phoneme-based multilingual pre-training and crosslingual fine-tuning is attractive for its high data efficiency and competitive results compared to subword-based models. However, Weighted Finite State Transducer (WFST) based decoding is limited by its complex pipeline and inability to leverage large language models (LLMs). Therefore, we propose LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme (LLM-P2G) decoding for phoneme-based ASR, consisting of speech-to-phoneme (S2P) and phoneme-to-grapheme (P2G). A challenge is that there seems to have information loss in cascading S2P and P2G. To address this challenge, we propose two training strategies: data augmentation with noisy phonemes (DANP), and randomized top-$K$ marginalized (TKM) training and decoding. Our experimental results show that LLM-P2G outperforms WFST-based systems in crosslingual ASR for Polish and German, by relative WER reductions of 3.6% and 6.9% respectively.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the paper "LLM-based phoneme-to-grapheme for phoneme-based speech recognition."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach called LLM-P2G for phoneme-based Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR). It addresses the limitations of traditional WFST-based decoding by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for the phoneme-to-grapheme (P2G) conversion step. The architecture consists of a Speech-to-Phoneme (S2P) model (Whistle-S2P) followed by an LLM-P2G model. To combat the issue of information loss between the S2P and P2G stages, the authors propose two training strategies: Data Augmentation with Noisy Phonemes (DANP) and Randomized Top-K Marginalized (TKM) training and decoding. The experimental results on crosslingual ASR for Polish and German demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods, showing improvements over WFST-based systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of existing techniques within the context of low-resource multilingual phoneme-based ASR. While the idea of using LLMs for ASR is not entirely new, the application to phoneme-to-grapheme conversion, particularly in a two-step architecture, and the proposed DANP and TKM strategies for mitigating information loss, contribute to the novelty of the work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** The paper tackles the challenge of leveraging LLMs in phoneme-based ASR, which is relevant for low-resource languages where subword-based approaches may suffer from data scarcity.
    *   **Clear Architecture and Methods:** The architecture (S2P + LLM-P2G) is clearly defined, and the proposed DANP and TKM strategies are well-explained.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results on Polish and German demonstrate the effectiveness of LLM-P2G in improving crosslingual ASR performance, with statistically significant gains using randomized TKM.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of different components, helping to understand the mechanisms behind the observed improvements.
    *   **Open Sourced Code and Models:** The availability of code and models promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The core idea of using an LLM as a language model replacement isn't revolutionary. This implementation is effective, but not ground breaking.
    *   **Limited Generalizability Evidence:** While the experiments are convincing for Polish and German, the generalizability to other languages, especially those with significantly different phonetic structures or writing systems, is not thoroughly explored. The reliance on the mT5's existing tokenization constrains that possibility.
    *   **Dependency on S2P Quality:** The performance of the LLM-P2G system heavily depends on the accuracy of the S2P model. Errors in the phoneme sequence from S2P can propagate and negatively impact the P2G conversion. This dependence is mitigated, but not fully resolved, by DANP and TKM.
    *   **Complexity:** While simpler than a complete WFST pipeline, this pipeline is still complex, and potentially cumbersome to deploy and scale up.
    *   **Comparison to strong LM baselines:** The use of a 4-gram LM is fairly weak in the current ASR landscape. It would have been stronger to also compare with a strong neural LM baseline to further validate the benefit of this approach.

**Significance:** The paper contributes to the ongoing research on leveraging LLMs for ASR, particularly in the context of low-resource languages. The proposed LLM-P2G architecture and the DANP/TKM training strategies provide practical techniques for improving phoneme-based ASR systems. The demonstrated improvements over traditional WFST-based decoding suggest that LLM-based approaches hold promise for future ASR development. The open-source nature of the work further enhances its significance by enabling other researchers to build upon the findings.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of ASR, but lacks the groundbreaking nature required for a higher score. While the LLM-P2G approach with DANP/TKM is effective in improving performance in crosslingual ASR, it builds upon existing techniques and has limitations in terms of generalizability and reliance on S2P quality. The novelty is moderate, but the empirical validation and ablation studies support the effectiveness of the proposed methods. The impact on the field is likely to be incremental, providing a practical approach for leveraging LLMs in phoneme-based ASR, rather than revolutionizing the field. It would have been better with a strong LM to compare against, and a wider range of languages tested.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### Towards Holistic Visual Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Videos: A LLM-Based Multi-Dimensional Evaluation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04715v1)
- **Authors**: Zelu Qi, Ping Shi, Chaoyang Zhang, Shuqi Wang, Fei Zhao, Da Pan, Zefeng Ying
- **Abstract**: The development of AI-Generated Video (AIGV) technology has been remarkable in recent years, significantly transforming the paradigm of video content production. However, AIGVs still suffer from noticeable visual quality defects, such as noise, blurriness, frame jitter and low dynamic degree, which severely impact the user's viewing experience. Therefore, an effective automatic visual quality assessment is of great importance for AIGV content regulation and generative model improvement. In this work, we decompose the visual quality of AIGVs into three dimensions: technical quality, motion quality, and video semantics. For each dimension, we design corresponding encoder to achieve effective feature representation. Moreover, considering the outstanding performance of large language models (LLMs) in various vision and language tasks, we introduce a LLM as the quality regression module. To better enable the LLM to establish reasoning associations between multi-dimensional features and visual quality, we propose a specially designed multi-modal prompt engineering framework. Additionally, we incorporate LoRA fine-tuning technology during the training phase, allowing the LLM to better adapt to specific tasks. Our proposed method achieved \textbf{second place} in the NTIRE 2025 Quality Assessment of AI-Generated Content Challenge: Track 2 AI Generated video, demonstrating its effectiveness. Codes can be obtained at https://github.com/QiZelu/AIGVEval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel LLM-based framework, AIGVEval, for assessing the visual quality of AI-generated videos (AIGVs). AIGVEval decomposes video quality into three dimensions: video semantics, technical quality, and motion quality.  It employs dedicated encoders for each dimension to extract relevant features. A key innovation is a multi-modal prompt engineering framework coupled with LoRA fine-tuning to enable the LLM to effectively reason about the multi-dimensional features and predict quality scores.  The method achieved second place in the NTIRE 2025 AI-Generated Content Challenge. Ablation studies validate the contribution of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing work leveraging LLMs for video quality assessment. While using LLMs for VQA isn't new, the authors' specific approach has several novel components. The *multi-dimensional decomposition* of video quality, with *dedicated encoders for each dimension*, is a structured way to represent the diverse aspects influencing perceptual quality. The *multi-modal prompt engineering framework* with semantic anchors is a significant contribution. These anchors help the LLM better understand the relationship between features extracted by encoders and quality metrics. The use of LoRA finetuning to adapt the LLM is well-justified and a practical optimization.

*   **Significance:** Accurate AIGV quality assessment is vital for content regulation and improving generative models. The framework offers a more holistic perspective than previous methods that often rely on generic video understanding encoders.  The prompt engineering contribution is crucial.  LLMs have immense potential, but carefully designed prompts are necessary to unlock that potential in specific tasks like VQA. The experimental results, particularly the second place in a competitive challenge, provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. This competitive result is significant and demonstrates the practical impact of the research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Novel and well-designed multi-dimensional feature extraction.
    *   Effective prompt engineering framework that leverages semantic anchors.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation including ablation studies and comparison against state-of-the-art methods.
    *   Success in a relevant competition which lends credibility to the practical applicability of the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the multi-dimensional approach is good, the specific choice of dimensions (semantics, technical, motion) could be further justified. Are these truly independent, and is this the optimal decomposition?
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the *types of errors* that the method still struggles with. Is it better at detecting certain artifacts than others?
    *   The paper mentions that their model shows only competitive performance in the context of the T2VQA-DB dataset, citing the inconsistency with textual prompts as the underlying reason. This could be treated as a motivation for further research and potential weakness.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more structured and interpretable framework for AIGV quality assessment. The prompt engineering techniques could be adopted in other LLM-based VQA tasks. The work encourages further research into how to best integrate different feature representations into LLMs for complex reasoning tasks.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed and demonstrably effective method for AIGV quality assessment. While it builds on existing LLM-based VQA work, its contributions, particularly the multi-dimensional approach, the custom encoders, and the prompt engineering framework, are novel and significant. The successful performance in a competition provides substantial evidence for its practical impact. The paper’s relatively modest score (7), is based on the fact that the *core* idea of using LLMs for VQA is already established, however the *specific* approach is innovative. The paper could be strengthened further by better justifying the dimensionality reduction, addressing weaknesses, and conducting further error analysis. The paper needs additional research to fully unlock the potential in a wide variety of cases.

### Learning dissection trajectories from expert surgical videos via imitation learning with equivariant diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04716v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyu Wang, Yonghao Long, Yueyao Chen, Hon-Chi Yip, Markus Scheppach, Philip Wai-Yan Chiu, Yeung Yam, Helen Mei-Ling Meng, Qi Dou
- **Abstract**: Endoscopic Submucosal Dissection (ESD) is a well-established technique for removing epithelial lesions. Predicting dissection trajectories in ESD videos offers significant potential for enhancing surgical skill training and simplifying the learning process, yet this area remains underexplored. While imitation learning has shown promise in acquiring skills from expert demonstrations, challenges persist in handling uncertain future movements, learning geometric symmetries, and generalizing to diverse surgical scenarios. To address these, we introduce a novel approach: Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations for Imitation Learning (iDPOE). Our method models expert behavior through a joint state action distribution, capturing the stochastic nature of dissection trajectories and enabling robust visual representation learning across various endoscopic views. By incorporating a diffusion model into policy learning, iDPOE ensures efficient training and sampling, leading to more accurate predictions and better generalization. Additionally, we enhance the model's ability to generalize to geometric symmetries by embedding equivariance into the learning process. To address state mismatches, we develop a forward-process guided action inference strategy for conditional sampling. Using an ESD video dataset of nearly 2000 clips, experimental results show that our approach surpasses state-of-the-art methods, both explicit and implicit, in trajectory prediction. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first application of imitation learning to surgical skill development for dissection trajectory prediction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Learning Dissection Trajectories from Expert Surgical Videos via Imitation Learning with Equivariant Diffusion" introduces a novel approach, Implicit Diffusion Policy with Equivariant Representations for Imitation Learning (iDPOE), to predict dissection trajectories in endoscopic submucosal dissection (ESD) surgery.  It tackles the challenges of uncertainty in future movements, geometric symmetries, and generalization to diverse surgical scenarios by using an implicit diffusion policy learned from expert video demonstrations.  The key innovations involve using a diffusion model to implicitly represent expert behaviors, incorporating equivariance to handle geometric transformations (rotations), and developing a forward-process guided action inference strategy to ensure consistency during sampling. The method is evaluated on a collected ESD video dataset and demonstrates improved performance compared to state-of-the-art imitation learning techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits a decent level of novelty. The combination of an implicit diffusion policy, equivariant representations, and a forward-process guidance strategy for trajectory prediction in the context of ESD surgery is a relatively unexplored area. Existing works have explored imitation learning and surgical scene understanding, but few have specifically addressed dissection trajectory prediction with the nuances of geometric symmetries and trajectory uncertainty present in endoscopic videos. However, each of these components has been used in isolation or in other combinations in different fields. The key novelty resides in the specific application and integration of these techniques to address the problem of dissection trajectory prediction.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance of this work is moderate. Accurate dissection trajectory prediction could lead to improved surgical training, reduced operative errors, and enhanced patient safety in ESD procedures. The paper's results suggest a promising step towards achieving these goals. However, the impact hinges on the method's ability to generalize to diverse surgical scenarios and ultimately translate into real-world clinical practice. The current evaluation, while thorough, is limited to a specific ESD dataset. Further validation on larger, more varied datasets and eventual clinical trials will be necessary to fully demonstrate its clinical relevance. The computational complexity must also be addressed to enable real-time deployment.
    The main weaknesses are:
    *   The improvement in performance is not dramatic. Other methods almost achieve the same results
    *   The benefits in robustness are not very noticeable, especially if we consider that the gap with other methods increases significantly when there is no blurring
    *   Real-time deployment and usability are still far away

    The strengths of this paper:

*   addresses a practical and impactful problem in surgical training and assistance
*   introduces a well-reasoned and technically sound approach
*   provides thorough experimental evaluation with comparisons to state-of-the-art methods

*   **Justification of Score:**
I assign a score of **6**. The paper presents a novel combination of techniques to address a relevant problem. The method exhibits promising results in comparison to existing approaches. However, the novelty is incremental, and the impact is limited by the need for further validation and real-time implementation challenges. The improvements are not dramatically superior to existing methods, and the generalization capabilities must be tested more thoroughly. A higher score would be warranted with more substantial improvements and a more convincing demonstration of practical applicability.
