The authors demonstrate that this approach yields lower perplexity in language modeling and better downstream performance, especially on tasks requiring long context, though at the cost of increased inference time flops.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the specific implementation of the Mesa layer: the chunkwise parallelization, the focus on numerical stability, and the way it dynamically allocates test-time compute. The idea of recurrent networks deriving from in-context regression is an extension of recent trends in the field (DeltaNet, Mamba), so the underlying *concept* is not entirely new. The authors do a great job situating their work in the context of related work, thoroughly explaining the connection to existing approaches. Also, the fact that a concurrent work, Atlas [10], corresponds exactly to a sliding-window variant of Mesa layer, weakens somewhat its novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution by:
    *   Showing a concrete way to achieve state-of-the-art performance in RNNs.
    *   Highlighting the crucial trade-off between training compute, test-time compute, and performance in sequence models. They show that dedicating more compute to inference can be beneficial.
    *   Providing a very thorough empirical evaluation, both on synthetic tasks and large-scale language modeling. This empirical rigour is particularly valuable for understanding the capabilities of modern RNN architectures.
    *   Identifying the shortcomings of modern RNNs in tasks with long contexts. This is a critical point and is very valuable in the field to avoid pushing RNNs where they are not suitable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** Addresses a real limitation of transformers (linear scaling of memory/compute during inference).
    *   **Solid Technical Approach:** The chunkwise parallelization and use of conjugate gradient solver are well-motivated and explained.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper includes a strong suite of experiments on synthetic tasks and a language modeling task. The benchmark is impressive and has led to important findings related to the limitations of RNNs.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The dissection of different benchmarks reveals hidden limitations of some recurrent models. The analysis regarding the number of CG steps per head and layer, is very valuable as well.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide sufficient detail in the paper and the appendix to facilitate understanding and potential reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Inference Cost:** The increased inference time is a notable drawback. While dynamic allocation is interesting, it does not completely solve the problem.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results are impressive, the gains over other recent RNN architectures are incremental.
    *   **Dependence on Heuristics:** The upper-bounding of forget gates seems somewhat ad-hoc and could potentially limit the model's ability to handle certain types of sequences. The current Mesa architecture implementation is fairly complicated, involving both numerous parameters and hyperparameters.
    *   **Limited theoretical justification.** There is no theoretical justification on when to use or not the CG stopping criterion to choose which instances require reallocating the test-time computation, but simply an experimental analysis.

*   **Potential Impact:**
    *   MesaNet could inspire new architectures that leverage optimal test-time training.
    *   The work provides a framework for reasoning about the trade-offs between different types of compute in sequence models.
    *   The analysis could guide future research on improving RNN architectures for long-context understanding.

*   **Justification for Score:**

Given the analysis, I assign a score of **7**. The paper presents a novel implementation of a recurrent architecture with strong empirical results and valuable analysis of the limitations of modern RNNs. While the basic concept of optimal test-time training has been explored previously, this paper makes a significant contribution by addressing scalability and numerical stability issues. The fact that a concurrent work, Atlas [10], corresponds exactly to a sliding-window variant of Mesa layer, diminishes somewhat its novelty. The increased inference time is a practical limitation, and the improvements over SOTA RNNs are relatively incremental, however, the analysis of the different types of compute in sequence models is very valuable. Overall, the thorough experimentation, clear writing, and important finding related to RNNs limitations makes this work a strong contribution within the sequence modeling field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Aligning Latent Spaces with Flow Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05240v1)
- **Authors**: Yizhuo Li, Yuying Ge, Yixiao Ge, Ying Shan, Ping Luo
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a novel framework for aligning learnable latent spaces to arbitrary target distributions by leveraging flow-based generative models as priors. Our method first pretrains a flow model on the target features to capture the underlying distribution. This fixed flow model subsequently regularizes the latent space via an alignment loss, which reformulates the flow matching objective to treat the latents as optimization targets. We formally prove that minimizing this alignment loss establishes a computationally tractable surrogate objective for maximizing a variational lower bound on the log-likelihood of latents under the target distribution. Notably, the proposed method eliminates computationally expensive likelihood evaluations and avoids ODE solving during optimization. As a proof of concept, we demonstrate in a controlled setting that the alignment loss landscape closely approximates the negative log-likelihood of the target distribution. We further validate the effectiveness of our approach through large-scale image generation experiments on ImageNet with diverse target distributions, accompanied by detailed discussions and ablation studies. With both theoretical and empirical validation, our framework paves a new way for latent space alignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for aligning the latent spaces of generative models to arbitrary target distributions.  It leverages pre-trained flow-based generative models as priors to regularize the latent space. The method involves two stages: first, a flow model is pre-trained on target features to capture the desired distribution. Then, this fixed flow model is used to define an alignment loss that regularizes the learnable latent space. The authors theoretically prove that minimizing this alignment loss effectively maximizes a variational lower bound on the log-likelihood of the latents under the target distribution. The method offers computational efficiency, avoiding expensive likelihood evaluations and ODE solving during optimization. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach through toy experiments and large-scale image generation tasks on ImageNet.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using pre-trained flow models as priors for latent space alignment is novel. Existing methods often rely on known distributions (e.g., Gaussian) or computationally expensive per-sample feature matching. The key innovation lies in reformulating flow matching as an alignment loss, where the latent space becomes the optimization target. The theoretical justification linking this alignment loss to a variational lower bound adds further depth and justifies the heuristic choice.
* **Significance:** The significance stems from several advantages offered by the framework. First, it addresses the limitations of traditional alignment methods by allowing alignment to arbitrary, even implicitly defined, target distributions. Second, the alignment loss is shown to be a computationally tractable proxy for the log-likelihood, avoiding the complexities of direct likelihood maximization. Finally, the framework is computationally lightweight compared to adversarial training or feature extraction-based approaches. If the proposed alignment method improves generative model performance or interpretability for real-world applications, its impact on the field will be substantial.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Theoretical Foundation:** The paper provides a solid theoretical grounding by connecting the alignment loss to a variational lower bound on the log-likelihood.
    * **Computational Efficiency:**  The method avoids computationally expensive likelihood evaluations and ODE solving, making it more practical than some alternatives.
    * **Flexibility:** The framework can align latent spaces to diverse target distributions, even those defined by samples.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents comprehensive experiments, including toy examples and large-scale image generation, to validate the effectiveness of the approach. The ablation studies further strengthen the analysis.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependence on Flow Model Quality:** The performance of the method heavily relies on the quality of the pre-trained flow model. A poorly trained flow model could lead to suboptimal alignment. The paper assumes optimality of the flow model, which is a strong assumption that may not always hold in practice.
    * **Potential Scalability Issues:** While the alignment loss is computationally tractable, the initial pre-training of the flow model on the target features could still be resource-intensive for very high-dimensional target distributions.
    * **Limited Comparative Analysis:** The paper lacks a direct comparison with state-of-the-art latent space alignment methods on standard benchmark datasets. This makes it difficult to assess the relative performance of the proposed framework. While comparisons with vanilla AEs and KLD regularization are included, a rigorous comparison against other recent alignment approaches will enhance the evaluation.
* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of generative modeling by providing a more flexible and efficient method for aligning latent spaces. It could be useful for improving the interpretability and controllability of generative models, as well as for incorporating domain-specific prior knowledge. Its lightweight nature could also make it attractive for resource-constrained applications.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of 7 seems appropriate. The paper introduces a conceptually novel and theoretically grounded approach to latent space alignment. The framework offers several advantages over existing methods, including flexibility, computational efficiency, and the ability to align to arbitrary target distributions. The comprehensive experimental validation supports the effectiveness of the approach. However, the paper also has some limitations, including the dependence on flow model quality, potential scalability issues, and lack of a direct comparison with state-of-the-art alignment methods. Therefore, while the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field, its impact may be limited by these factors.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SECNEURON: Reliable and Flexible Abuse Control in Local LLMs via Hybrid Neuron Encryption
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05242v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiqiang Wang, Haohua Du, Junyang Wang, Haifeng Sun, Kaiwen Guo, Haikuo Yu, Chao Liu, Xiang-Yang Li
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) with diverse capabilities are increasingly being deployed in local environments, presenting significant security and controllability challenges. These locally deployed LLMs operate outside the direct control of developers, rendering them more susceptible to abuse. Existing mitigation techniques mainly designed for cloud-based LLM services are frequently circumvented or ineffective in deployer-controlled environments. We propose SECNEURON, the first framework that seamlessly embeds classic access control within the intrinsic capabilities of LLMs, achieving reliable, cost-effective, flexible, and certified abuse control for local deployed LLMs. SECNEURON employs neuron-level encryption and selective decryption to dynamically control the task-specific capabilities of LLMs, limiting unauthorized task abuse without compromising others. We first design a task-specific neuron extraction mechanism to decouple logically related neurons and construct a layered policy tree for handling coupled neurons. We then introduce a flexible and efficient hybrid encryption framework for millions of neurons in LLMs. Finally, we developed a distribution-based decrypted neuron detection mechanism on ciphertext to ensure the effectiveness of partially decrypted LLMs. We proved that SECNEURON satisfies IND-CPA Security and Collusion Resistance Security under the Task Controllability Principle. Experiments on various task settings show that SECNEURON limits unauthorized task accuracy to below 25% while keeping authorized accuracy loss with 2%. Using an unauthorized Code task example, the accuracy of abuse-related malicious code generation was reduced from 59% to 15%. SECNEURON also mitigates unauthorized data leakage, reducing PII extraction rates to below 5% and membership inference to random guesses.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "SECNEURON: Reliable and Flexible Abuse Control in Local LLMs via Hybrid Neuron Encryption."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SECNEURON, a novel framework designed to control abuse in locally deployed large language models (LLMs). Unlike existing mitigation techniques primarily tailored for cloud-based LLM services, SECNEURON focuses on enabling fine-grained access control at the neuron level within the LLM itself.  The key ideas include: (1) Task-specific neuron extraction and decoupling to create a layered policy tree. This allows for more precise control without unduly affecting unrelated tasks. (2) A hybrid encryption framework that combines the flexibility of attribute-based encryption (CP-ABE) with the efficiency of symmetric encryption (AES-CTR). This allows for both fine-grained access control and acceptable performance.  (3) A distribution-based decrypted neuron detection mechanism to ensure the effectiveness of partially decrypted LLMs. This identifies neurons that weren't successfully decrypted and prunes them.  The authors claim that SECNEURON ensures reliable, cost-effective, flexible, and certifiable abuse control by limiting unauthorized task accuracy, mitigating data leakage, and supporting dynamic capability adjustments with minimal overhead. Experimental results seemingly validate these claims.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using neuron-level encryption for access control in LLMs is fairly novel. The combination of CP-ABE for key management and AES-CTR for efficient neuron encryption addresses a key challenge in applying cryptographic techniques to large models. The method for task-specific neuron extraction, while building on existing importance analysis, provides a dedicated decoupling mechanism suitable for local LLM permission granularity.  The decrypted neuron detection algorithm is also a pragmatic and necessary component to ensure the robustness of the approach in the face of decryption failures. However, some level of prior work exists in neuron importance selection and model pruning.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem: the lack of robust abuse control mechanisms for locally deployed LLMs. The trend of deploying LLMs in edge environments is growing, and existing cloud-based security measures are often inadequate in such scenarios.  SECNEURON offers a promising approach to enable more secure and trustworthy local LLM deployments.  The potential applications include secure commercial licensing, dynamic on-device permission control, and protection against unauthorized data extraction.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:**  SECNEURON tackles the problem with a well-defined, layered framework encompassing neuron selection, encryption, decryption, and performance optimization.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The design acknowledges the constraints of large models (millions of neurons) and aims for efficient and flexible deployment. The hybrid encryption scheme and decrypted neuron detection are key to this.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides experimental results across various tasks, models, and attack scenarios. The reported reductions in unauthorized task accuracy and data leakage rates are encouraging.
    *   **Security Analysis:**  The authors formally define a "Task Controllability Principle" and provide proofs of IND-CPA security and collusion resistance under this principle.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Task Controllability Principle:** The requirement that each task needs a sufficient number of unique task-specific neurons is a crucial assumption. While the authors propose techniques to encourage task-specific neuron activation, there is a theoretical limit to this as discussed in "Task Capacity Upper Bound" if neuron interdependency is significantly high. How this assumption holds for different LLM architectures and pre-training strategies is unclear and warrants further analysis. The paper may need more sophisticated neuron disentanglement methods beyond the penalty factor and embedding overlapping neurons in CP-ABE access trees.
    *   **Practical Deployment Overhead:** While the paper discusses overhead and provides detailed measurement, the actual end-to-end deployment complexity should be carefully considered in a real-world environment with dynamic and complex access policies. More discussion of how the key exchange and permission update processes would be integrated into practical systems would be valuable.
    *   **Reliance on Importance Metrics:** The performance of SECNEURON depends on the effectiveness of the neuron importance metric used to select task-specific neurons. While the authors use a mean of absolute activation, they acknowledge that other methods could be employed. The robustness of SECNEURON to adversarial attacks designed to manipulate the importance scores of neurons needs to be investigated.
    *   **Limited Threat Model Expansion:** This paper has limited threat model for side-channel attacks, like differential power analysis and timing attacks, especially during the decryption process. A more thorough security analysis should take into account a broader range of potential attacks.
    *   **Lack of detailed discussion of the limitations of the chosen cryptographic components**: The paper does not sufficiently address the limitation of the CP-ABE. For example, the efficiency limitations of CP-ABE schemes in scenarios with high policy complexity.

*   **Potential Influence:** SECNEURON has the potential to influence the development of more secure and controllable local LLM deployments. It could inspire new research directions in neuron-level access control and cryptographic techniques for large models. It will likely prompt further exploration of neuron disentanglement techniques and more robust methods for assessing neuron importance.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** SECNEURON represents a significant and novel contribution to the field by addressing a practical security problem in locally deployed LLMs. While the paper has limitations, particularly concerning the Task Controllability Principle's assumption and the potential overhead in real-world deployments, the core ideas are sound and well-executed. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach, and the security analysis provides a reasonable level of assurance. The potential influence of SECNEURON on future research in this area is considerable. A slightly higher score would require more compelling evidence that the Task Controllability Principle will hold in a wide range of real-world deployments, a more thorough analysis of practical deployment overhead, and additional defense against other threat models.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### On the Convergence of Gradient Descent on Learning Transformers with Residual Connections
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05249v1)
- **Authors**: Zhen Qin, Jinxin Zhou, Zhihui Zhu
- **Abstract**: Transformer models have emerged as fundamental tools across various scientific and engineering disciplines, owing to their outstanding performance in diverse applications. Despite this empirical success, the theoretical foundations of Transformers remain relatively underdeveloped, particularly in understanding their training dynamics. Existing research predominantly examines isolated components--such as self-attention mechanisms and feedforward networks--without thoroughly investigating the interdependencies between these components, especially when residual connections are present. In this paper, we aim to bridge this gap by analyzing the convergence behavior of a structurally complete yet single-layer Transformer, comprising self-attention, a feedforward network, and residual connections. We demonstrate that, under appropriate initialization, gradient descent exhibits a linear convergence rate, where the convergence speed is determined by the minimum and maximum singular values of the output matrix from the attention layer. Moreover, our analysis reveals that residual connections serve to ameliorate the ill-conditioning of this output matrix, an issue stemming from the low-rank structure imposed by the softmax operation, thereby promoting enhanced optimization stability. We also extend our theoretical findings to a multi-layer Transformer architecture, confirming the linear convergence rate of gradient descent under suitable initialization. Empirical results corroborate our theoretical insights, illustrating the beneficial role of residual connections in promoting convergence stability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a theoretical analysis of the convergence behavior of gradient descent when training single-layer and multi-layer Transformer models with self-attention, feedforward networks, and residual connections. The authors demonstrate that under appropriate initialization, gradient descent exhibits a linear convergence rate.  The convergence speed is related to the minimum and maximum singular values of the output matrix from the attention layer.  A key finding is that residual connections help ameliorate the ill-conditioning of this output matrix, a problem caused by the low-rank nature of the softmax operation, thus promoting optimization stability.  Empirical results on a real-world weather dataset support the theoretical findings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Gap:** The paper tackles a significant gap in the theoretical understanding of Transformers. While many studies focus on isolated components, this work analyzes the interdependencies between self-attention, feedforward networks, and *crucially*, residual connections, within a complete Transformer architecture. The inclusion of residual connections is a major step forward.
    *   **Rigorous Analysis:** The analysis appears mathematically rigorous. The paper provides theorems, proofs, and assumptions that are well-defined. The use of matrix perturbation theory seems appropriate for the problem.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The focus on gradient descent is highly relevant since it's the dominant optimization method for training Transformers.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical results, though limited to a single dataset, provide some initial validation of the theoretical findings. The use of a real-world dataset adds to the impact.
    *   **Ill-conditioning Problem Tackled**: Explicitly addresses the rank collapse problem and how residual connections can help.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simplifications:** The analysis relies on significant simplifications. The single-layer Transformer, while structurally complete, is not representative of the massive, deep Transformers used in practice. The absence of layer normalization is also a limitation, given its ubiquity in modern Transformers. The multi-layer extension is useful, but relies heavily on the single-layer case analysis.
    *   **Strong Assumptions:**  The initialization assumptions might be difficult to satisfy in practice. Specifically, the full-rank assumptions on the weight matrices and the strict conditions on the singular values significantly limit the practical relevance of the analysis. The Lipschitz condition, while common, also restricts the activation functions that can be used.
    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** The empirical section is weak. A single dataset and limited hyperparameter exploration make it difficult to generalize the results. More diverse datasets and larger models would strengthen the empirical claim.
    *   **Lack of Generalization from Theory:** It isn't entirely clear how the theoretical conditions directly translate to practical guidelines for initialization or residual connection design.  The paper highlights the *importance* of residual connections, but doesn't provide actionable insights on *how* to best leverage them, *quantitatively*, in real-world scenarios.
    *   **Limited novelty:** convergence proofs exist for a number of neural networks. While this adds residual connections and focuses on transformers it isn't significantly novel. The technical approaches seem like a fairly standard application of techniques in the space.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    While the paper makes a contribution to understanding the training dynamics of Transformers, the novelty is somewhat incremental. The analysis combines existing techniques to address a practically relevant question (the role of residual connections). The simplifications and assumptions, however, limit the scope and impact of the findings. While showing convergence with the given constraints is valuable, the impact is limited if the constraints are difficult to apply.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper could influence future research by encouraging more theoretical work that considers complete Transformer architectures, including the interplay of different components. It also highlights the importance of residual connections, which could prompt further investigation into their design and optimization. The result helps improve understanding but isn't paradigm shifting.

**Score: 5/10**

**Justification:**

The paper provides a theoretically sound but incremental contribution to the understanding of Transformer training dynamics. It addresses an important gap by explicitly analyzing residual connections in the context of complete (albeit simplified) Transformer layers. However, strong assumptions and limited empirical validation weaken the practical relevance and impact of the findings. The techniques are well known and there is limited novelty in these application, even though the overall result is still valuable. While the paper offers some insights, the simplifications and assumptions make it a moderate contribution at best, and less likely to have a transformative impact on the field. A more comprehensive empirical study and relaxation of some of the strong assumptions would substantially increase the value of this work.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 5/10

### LeanPO: Lean Preference Optimization for Likelihood Alignment in Video-LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05260v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaodong Wang, Jinfa Huang, Li Yuan, Peixi Peng
- **Abstract**: Most Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) adopt preference alignment techniques, e.g., DPO~\citep{rafailov2024dpo}, to optimize the reward margin between a winning response ($y_w$) and a losing response ($y_l$). However, the likelihood displacement observed in DPO indicates that both $\log \pi_\theta (y_w\mid x)$ and $\log \pi_\theta (y_l\mid x) $ often decrease during training, inadvertently boosting the probabilities of non-target responses. In this paper, we systematically revisit this phenomenon from LLMs to Video-LLMs, showing that it intensifies when dealing with the redundant complexity of video content. To alleviate the impact of this phenomenon, we propose \emph{Lean Preference Optimization} (LeanPO), a reference-free approach that reformulates the implicit reward as the average likelihood of the response with respect to the policy model. A key component of LeanPO is the reward-trustworthiness correlated self-generated preference data pipeline, which carefully infuses relevant prior knowledge into the model while continuously refining the preference data via self-reflection. This allows the policy model to obtain high-quality paired data and accurately estimate the newly defined reward, thus mitigating the unintended drop. In addition, we introduce a dynamic label smoothing strategy that mitigates the impact of noise in responses from diverse video content, preventing the model from overfitting to spurious details. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LeanPO significantly enhances the performance of state-of-the-art Video-LLMs, consistently boosting baselines of varying capacities with minimal additional training overhead. Moreover, LeanPO offers a simple yet effective solution for aligning Video-LLM preferences with human trustworthiness, paving the way toward the reliable and efficient Video-LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LEANPO paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LEANPO: Lean Preference Optimization for Likelihood Alignment in Video-LLMs" addresses the issue of likelihood displacement observed during preference alignment (using Direct Preference Optimization, DPO) in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs).  The authors argue that the inherent complexity and redundancy of video content exacerbate this problem, leading to instability in reward signals and inefficient training. To counter this, they propose Lean Preference Optimization (LEANPO), a reference-free approach that reformulates the reward as the average likelihood of the response with respect to the policy model. A crucial part of LEANPO is a pipeline for generating reward-trustworthiness correlated self-generated preference data, coupled with a dynamic label smoothing strategy to handle noisy video content.  Experiments show LEANPO improving the performance of Video-LLMs on several benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in identifying and systematically addressing the likelihood displacement problem within the specific context of *Video-LLMs*.  While likelihood displacement is known in LLMs, the authors demonstrate its amplified impact when dealing with video. The proposed LEANPO framework combines several elements: a reference-free reward formulation, a tailored data generation pipeline that aims to balance reward likelihood with trustworthiness, and a dynamic label smoothing technique. The combination of these isn't entirely groundbreaking on their own, but their application and synergy within the Video-LLM domain appears novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance hinges on whether LEANPO provides a tangible improvement over existing alignment methods, especially DPO, for Video-LLMs. The results demonstrate consistent improvements across several benchmarks, which suggests that LEANPO is a more effective alignment approach. If the method enables more reliable and trustworthy Video-LLMs with minimal overhead it has a significant practical contribution. Furthermore, by highlighting the likelihood displacement issue, the paper encourages more research into alignment strategies specifically designed for multi-modal models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Identification:** Clear articulation and systematic investigation of the likelihood displacement issue in Video-LLMs.
    *   **Targeted Solution:** The LEANPO framework addresses the identified problem with a combination of components tailored to the challenges of video understanding.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Extensive experiments on diverse video benchmarks demonstrating the effectiveness of LEANPO over baselines, including DPO.
    *   **Practicality:** LEANPO achieves significant performance gains with only small additional training overhead, enhancing scalability for larger Video-LLMs.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and provides a detailed explanation of the method, making it easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Nature:** While the combination of elements within LEANPO is novel, the individual components (self-generated data, label smoothing) are not entirely new in the broader LLM alignment space. The novelty relies heavily on the specific application and tuning within Video-LLMs.
    *   **Limited Ablation:** More detailed ablation studies, breaking down the individual contributions of the data generation pipeline and dynamic label smoothing, would strengthen the claims. While some ablation is included, further isolating the effects of each component is necessary.
    *   **Overclaiming:** Claims about "reliable and efficient Video-LLMs" should be tempered. While LEANPO improves performance, it doesn't guarantee perfect reliability or eliminate all biases.
    *   **Limited comparison with other video methods**: The paper focuses on DPO as the main comparative baseline. It should compare and contrast more clearly with other video-specific alignment methods (beyond just mentioning them).

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   LEANPO could become a standard alignment technique for Video-LLMs, especially in resource-constrained settings.
    *   The paper may inspire further research into likelihood-aware alignment strategies tailored for multi-modal data.
    *   The reward-trustworthiness data generation pipeline could influence future methods of creating training data for Video-LLMs.

*   **Rigorous rationale for assigned score**: Although LEANPO addresses a key problem in Video-LLM alignment with a tailored and effective technique, the individual parts that make up LEANPO are mostly incremental. It's not a breakthrough in preference alignment. A strong improvement in benchmarks and empirical validations shows its significant utility, warranting a more favorable score but still somewhat limited in the underlying innovation.

Score: 7.0

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Teaming in the AI Era: AI-Augmented Frameworks for Forming, Simulating, and Optimizing Human Teams
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05265v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammed Almutairi
- **Abstract**: Effective teamwork is essential across diverse domains. During the team formation stage, a key challenge is forming teams that effectively balance user preferences with task objectives to enhance overall team satisfaction. In the team performing stage, maintaining cohesion and engagement is critical for sustaining high team performance. However, existing computational tools and algorithms for team optimization often rely on static data inputs, narrow algorithmic objectives, or solutions tailored for specific contexts, failing to account for the dynamic interplay of team members personalities, evolving goals, and changing individual preferences. Therefore, teams may encounter member dissatisfaction, as purely algorithmic assignments can reduce members commitment to team goals or experience suboptimal engagement due to the absence of timely, personalized guidance to help members adjust their behaviors and interactions as team dynamics evolve. Ultimately, these challenges can lead to reduced overall team performance. My Ph.D. dissertation aims to develop AI-augmented team optimization frameworks and practical systems that enhance team satisfaction, engagement, and performance. First, I propose a team formation framework that leverages a multi-armed bandit algorithm to iteratively refine team composition based on user preferences, ensuring alignment between individual needs and collective team goals to enhance team satisfaction. Second, I introduce tAIfa (Team AI Feedback Assistant), an AI-powered system that utilizes large language models (LLMs) to deliver immediate, personalized feedback to both teams and individual members, enhancing cohesion and engagement. Finally, I present PuppeteerLLM, an LLM-based simulation framework that simulates multi-agent teams to model complex team dynamics within realistic environments, incorporating task-driven collaboration and long-term coordination.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a PhD dissertation proposal focused on enhancing human team dynamics using AI-augmented frameworks. The dissertation addresses three key challenges: team formation, team engagement, and team dynamics simulation.  The proposed solutions involve: 1) a multi-armed bandit-based team formation framework that considers user preferences, 2) tAlfa, an LLM-powered system providing personalized feedback to teams, and 3) PuppeteerLLM, an LLM-based simulation framework for modeling complex team interactions. The goal is to improve team satisfaction, engagement, and performance.  The paper outlines research objectives, methodology, and preliminary results for some components, alongside plans for future work.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper tackles an important and relevant problem: how to optimize human teamwork in the age of AI.  The proposal leverages recent advances in AI, particularly LLMs and reinforcement learning, to address limitations in traditional team formation and management techniques.

Here's a critical evaluation:

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The dissertation proposal addresses multiple aspects of team dynamics, from formation to performance enhancement and simulation.
    *   **Leveraging LLMs:** The use of LLMs for personalized feedback and realistic simulation is promising and aligns with current trends in AI research.
    *   **Practical Application:** The development of practical systems like tAlfa and PuppeteerLLM demonstrates a commitment to real-world impact.
    *   **Clear Research Questions:** The research questions are well-defined, providing a clear direction for the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the application of LLMs to team dynamics is interesting, the core techniques (multi-armed bandits, agent-based modeling) are not entirely novel. The novelty lies primarily in the integration and application of these existing techniques in a specific context.
    *   **Limited Preliminary Results:**  The preliminary results for tAlfa are promising, but more details are needed to assess their significance. The paper lacks detail on the evaluation metrics and statistical significance of the findings.
    *   **Over-Reliance on LLMs:**  The dissertation appears to rely heavily on the capabilities of LLMs. The paper doesn't thoroughly address the potential limitations of LLMs, such as biases, hallucination, or difficulty in modeling complex social dynamics.
    *   **Evaluation Rigor:** The planned evaluation methods are somewhat generic. A more detailed discussion of the evaluation metrics, experimental design (e.g., controls for confounding variables), and statistical power analysis would strengthen the proposal.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to Baselines:** The paper does not clearly delineate what the baseline methodologies will be and why the proposed methods will surpass them. The multi-armed bandit framework is a standard technique, but does not provide sufficient detail on the modifications or potential advances the algorithm provides over existing methodologies.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The work has the potential to improve team performance and satisfaction in various domains.
    *   The LLM-based simulation framework could provide valuable insights into team dynamics and inform the design of better team interventions.
    *   The research could contribute to the broader field of human-AI collaboration.

**Overall Assessment:**

The proposal presents a worthwhile research direction. However, the level of novelty is moderate. The main contribution seems to be in the specific combination and application of existing techniques, rather than groundbreaking theoretical advances. The paper also lacks detailed discussion on many limitations of the LLMs that would be critical for a successful implementation.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The proposal scores a 6 because it identifies an important problem and proposes relevant AI-driven solutions. However, the novelty is incremental, and the proposal would benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations and a more rigorous evaluation plan. The reliance on LLMs, while promising, introduces risks that need to be carefully considered. The lack of explicit comparison to potential baselines raises concern that the improvements might be marginal.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Micro-Act: Mitigate Knowledge Conflict in Question Answering via Actionable Self-Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05278v1)
- **Authors**: Nan Huo, Jinyang Li, Bowen Qin, Ge Qu, Xiaolong Li, Xiaodong Li, Chenhao Ma, Reynold Cheng
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems commonly suffer from Knowledge Conflicts, where retrieved external knowledge contradicts the inherent, parametric knowledge of large language models (LLMs). It adversely affects performance on downstream tasks such as question answering (QA). Existing approaches often attempt to mitigate conflicts by directly comparing two knowledge sources in a side-by-side manner, but this can overwhelm LLMs with extraneous or lengthy contexts, ultimately hindering their ability to identify and mitigate inconsistencies. To address this issue, we propose Micro-Act a framework with a hierarchical action space that automatically perceives context complexity and adaptively decomposes each knowledge source into a sequence of fine-grained comparisons. These comparisons are represented as actionable steps, enabling reasoning beyond the superficial context. Through extensive experiments on five benchmark datasets, Micro-Act consistently achieves significant increase in QA accuracy over state-of-the-art baselines across all 5 datasets and 3 conflict types, especially in temporal and semantic types where all baselines fail significantly. More importantly, Micro-Act exhibits robust performance on non-conflict questions simultaneously, highlighting its practical value in real-world RAG applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MICRO-ACT, a novel framework designed to mitigate knowledge conflicts in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems for Question Answering (QA). Knowledge conflicts arise when retrieved external knowledge contradicts the Large Language Model's (LLM) internal, parametric knowledge. MICRO-ACT addresses this issue by employing a hierarchical action space that allows the LLM to automatically perceive the complexity of the context and adaptively decompose knowledge sources into fine-grained, actionable comparisons. This allows for more in-depth reasoning beyond simple, superficial comparisons. The paper presents experimental results on five benchmark datasets, demonstrating MICRO-ACT's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art baselines, especially in temporal and semantic conflict scenarios. The method also maintains robust performance in non-conflict situations, showcasing its practical value. The paper further analyzes the dynamics of the model, showing that the framework adapts to complexity and can mitigate "over-rationalization."

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the hierarchical action space and the dynamic decomposition of knowledge for conflict resolution. While existing methods often rely on side-by-side comparisons or fine-tuning, MICRO-ACT provides a structured and adaptive way for LLMs to navigate and reconcile conflicting information. This is a significant departure from previous approaches. The paper also introduces the interesting phenomenon of "over-rationalization" and proposes a way to mitigate it.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is substantial. Knowledge conflicts are a critical barrier to the reliable deployment of RAG systems in real-world applications. MICRO-ACT addresses this directly, significantly improving QA accuracy, particularly in challenging conflict types. The model's ability to maintain performance in conflict-free scenarios is also highly relevant, as it makes the approach more practical for general use.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper demonstrates consistent and significant improvements across multiple datasets and conflict types.
    *   **Adaptive Granularity:** The framework dynamically adjusts its granularity to identify potential conflicting facts, which is a crucial benefit of this approach.
    *   **Robustness:** The model shows good performance in both conflict and conflict-free scenarios, which is a key requirement for practical deployment.
    *   **Analysis of Over-Rationalization:** The paper identifies a problem that has not been well discussed previously and develops mitigation strategies.
    *   **Structured Approach:** The hierarchical action space provides a clear and interpretable structure for knowledge conflict resolution.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational cost:**  As acknowledged by the authors, MICRO-ACT incurs additional computational costs, particularly in terms of token usage and inference time, due to the decomposition process. While the authors argue that this cost is acceptable given the performance gains, it is a limitation that needs to be considered.
    *   **Limited generalizability analysis:**  The evaluation focuses primarily on English language datasets. The paper briefly mentions that the effectiveness of decomposition strategies might vary across different languages and cultural contexts, but this is not explored empirically. The experiments are done with only a few variations of LLMs, restricting the generalizability claims.
    *   **Error Analysis is somewhat limited:** While the paper presents a reasonable error analysis, a deeper qualitative examination of failure cases and the types of conflicts where the adaptive methods don't work very well would strengthen the analysis further.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MICRO-ACT has the potential to significantly influence the design of future RAG systems. The hierarchical action space and dynamic decomposition approach could be adopted and extended by other researchers to address knowledge conflicts and other challenges in information retrieval and knowledge integration. The approach can also lead to more robust and reliable deployment of LLMs in real-world applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the increased computational cost and limited generalizability temper the evaluation, the work presents a novel and potentially transformative approach to a significant problem in the field of LLMs and RAG. The empirical evidence is compelling, and the analysis provides valuable insights into the behavior of the model and the nature of knowledge conflicts. The work goes beyond simply improving accuracy and offers an intriguing framework for structuring the reasoning process in LLMs.  The impact could be high, but there are definite avenues for further research and improvements that need to be made.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Stable Vision Concept Transformers for Medical Diagnosis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05286v1)
- **Authors**: Lijie Hu, Songning Lai, Yuan Hua, Shu Yang, Jingfeng Zhang, Di Wang
- **Abstract**: Transparency is a paramount concern in the medical field, prompting researchers to delve into the realm of explainable AI (XAI). Among these XAI methods, Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) aim to restrict the model's latent space to human-understandable high-level concepts by generating a conceptual layer for extracting conceptual features, which has drawn much attention recently. However, existing methods rely solely on concept features to determine the model's predictions, which overlook the intrinsic feature embeddings within medical images. To address this utility gap between the original models and concept-based models, we propose Vision Concept Transformer (VCT). Furthermore, despite their benefits, CBMs have been found to negatively impact model performance and fail to provide stable explanations when faced with input perturbations, which limits their application in the medical field. To address this faithfulness issue, this paper further proposes the Stable Vision Concept Transformer (SVCT) based on VCT, which leverages the vision transformer (ViT) as its backbone and incorporates a conceptual layer. SVCT employs conceptual features to enhance decision-making capabilities by fusing them with image features and ensures model faithfulness through the integration of Denoised Diffusion Smoothing. Comprehensive experiments on four medical datasets demonstrate that our VCT and SVCT maintain accuracy while remaining interpretable compared to baselines. Furthermore, even when subjected to perturbations, our SVCT model consistently provides faithful explanations, thus meeting the needs of the medical field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Stable Vision Concept Transformer (SVCT) for medical image diagnosis. SVCT aims to provide both accurate and interpretable predictions, addressing the limitations of Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs), which often suffer from performance degradation and instability under input perturbations. The core idea is to integrate conceptual features with image features from a Vision Transformer (ViT) backbone, using a label-free concept generation method. A Denoised Diffusion Smoothing (DDS) technique is incorporated to ensure stable interpretations even with noisy inputs. The paper presents experimental results on four medical datasets, demonstrating that SVCT maintains accuracy while providing interpretable and stable explanations, thus addressing a crucial need in the medical field.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper exhibits a moderate level of novelty.  The combination of label-free CBMs with ViTs for medical imaging is an interesting starting point. The primary novelty lies in the DDS smoothing applied to the conceptual layer to enhance stability and faithfulness of the explanations under perturbation. While ViTs and CBMs have been explored before, the specific integration with DDS for *stability* is a distinct contribution. Furthermore, the formal definition of "stable concept module" provides a valuable theoretical contribution.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed – lack of transparency and robustness of medical image AI – is highly significant.  The medical field demands trust and reliability, which hinge on interpretability and stable explanations. SVCT takes a step in this direction. The experimental results demonstrate a tangible improvement in stability and faithfulness without drastic sacrifices in accuracy, making it potentially useful for clinical applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear Problem Definition: The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing CBMs in the context of medical image analysis, particularly their instability and performance degradation.
    *   Well-Defined Method: The SVCT architecture is well-explained, with a clear rationale for each component (ViT backbone, label-free concept generation, DDS).
    *   Rigorous Evaluation:  The paper includes comprehensive experiments on multiple datasets and metrics (accuracy, CFS, CPCS), considering various perturbation levels.
    *   Theoretical Justification:  The paper provides a theoretical analysis and a formal definition of stable VCTs and DDS smoothing.
    *   Code and Data availability: Source code availability is often vital to increase utility to real-world practitioners.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Perturbation Types:  The paper primarily focuses on Gaussian noise as a perturbation. While this is a common type of noise, medical images are often subject to different artifacts and adversarial attacks. The study could benefit from evaluating robustness against other types of perturbations (e.g., occlusion, blurring, adversarial examples).
    *   Limited Generalizability Analysis: The reported standard deviations of the results are needed. This leads to some concerns regarding the real effect size when comparing baselines with SVCT.
    *   Human Evaluation of Interpretability: The interpretability metrics (CFS, CPCS) are proxy measures. A human evaluation study involving medical experts would provide more concrete evidence that the conceptual explanations are actually useful and understandable.
    *   Computational Cost: Although the compute cost is provided, its impact to real-world usage may limit wider adoption.

*   **Potential Impact:**  SVCT has the potential to influence the development of more trustworthy and transparent AI systems for medical image analysis. By providing stable and interpretable explanations, SVCT can facilitate better collaboration between clinicians and AI systems, leading to improved diagnostic accuracy and patient care. However, the limitations mentioned above need to be addressed before widespread adoption can occur. The introduced theoretical framework can also motivate further research into stable and faithful explanations in other domains.

**Justification for Score:**

SVCT presents a valuable advancement in the field of explainable AI for medical imaging.  It offers a practical solution to address the instability and performance degradation issues associated with traditional CBMs, particularly when applied to medical images. While it does have limitations, its strengths warrant a fairly high score.
